Um, well, no... I don't think it's exactly the same pattern every single time. It's more just... a general tapping, you know? Like I'm just touching the register a few times to make sure everything is... aligned.

Um, no, I wouldn't call it an urge. It's not like a... a compulsion or anything like that. I don't feel this big buildup of tension that I have to release.

Oh, um, no. No, it's not like I'm... reacting to other people or anything like that. If someone mentions it, I just get a little embarrassed, but it doesn't make me do it more. 

Like I was saying before, it's just that I get, well, stuck doing these little things at the register that don't really fit the moment. Like the tapping the scanner in a little pattern, or just... freezing with my hand over the cash drawer while the customer is waiting. It just happens on its own, not because of anyone else. 

I know I already mentioned that, and that it got me written up last week... I'm sorry if I'm repeating myself, I just want to make sure I'm answering your questions right. I really don't want you to think I'm, like, mimicking people or being weird on purpose. It's just a little stress habit, right? I'm hoping you see it that way too.

I hope I'm giving you the right details.\n\nDoctor: Right before one of these tapping or freezing episodes, do you feel a physical urge or buildup of tension that goes away once it happens?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I don't feel any kind of... tightness or tension building up inside me. It's not like a sneeze or an itch that I have to scratch, I promise. \n\nIt just... happens, kind of out of nowhere. I don't feel any sort of physical relief afterward, mostly just... well, mostly just embarrassed that I held up the line or made a customer wait. There's no buildup or release at all. \n\nI really hope I'm explaining this right for you. Does that make sense? I just want to be as accurate as possible so I can get this sorted out and get back to being a reliable employee.\n\nDoctor: During these tapping or freezing episodes, do you ever lose track of what's happening around you or have a blank spell?\n\nPatient: No, no, I don't lose track of things. I'm definitely still... still there, you know? I don't have blank spells or anything like that, I promise. \n\nI know exactly what's happening around me. I can see the customer, I can see the register... I just can't always move my hand right then. But my mind is completely clear, I'm not spacing out or forgetting where I am. I'm very focused on my job, even when my body isn't cooperating. \n\nI hope that doesn't make me sound too strange. I just want to make sure you know I'm still paying attention to my work. Is that the kind of information you needed?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever get unwanted, repetitive thoughts or images that keep coming back into your mind?\n\nPatient: Um... no, I don't think so. I don't really have anything like that. \n\nMy thoughts are usually pretty normal, I think. I just focus on my work and trying to be polite to the customers, like a good cashier should. I don't have any strange images or... or things repeating in my head that I don't want there. I promise my mind is very clear and focused on doing a good job. \n\nI wouldn't want you to think I'm losing my mind or anything like that. I'm just a regular person trying to do his best. Does... does that sound okay? I hope I'm being helpful.\n\nDoctor: When you’re at work, do you also have trouble staying focused or finish tasks because your mind wanders?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, definitely not. I don't let my mind wander when I'm on the clock. I actually pride myself on being very attentive at the register. I really try to be a model employee, you know? I wouldn't want my manager or anyone to think I'm slacking off or not paying attention. \n\nI hope that's a good thing? I just want to reassure you that my work ethic is still very strong, and I'm taking all of this seriously. Does that answer your question okay?\n\nDoctor: Can you hold back the tapping or freezing for a little while if you try?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I try really hard to. I don't think I can really stop it once it starts. It just sort of... happens, even when I'm telling myself to stop and just scan the next item. \n\nI really hope that doesn't make it sound like I'm not trying my best. I hope I'm answering your questions the right way.\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes happen as sudden, brief movements or sounds that repeat in the same way each time?\n\nPatient: Um, no... definitely not sounds. I think we might have just talked about that a minute ago, actually. But, um, no, there's no buildup or pressure or anything like that. \n\nIt's really not like an urge I have to satisfy. I promise I'm not feeling some weird tension that forces me to do it. It's just... I'm working, and then it just sort of happens. Like a little pause. \n\nI'm sorry, I hope I'm not frustrating you by giving the same answer. I just want to be sure I'm being clear that I'm not, you know, out of control or anything. It's really just a minor thing. Am I making sense? I'm trying my best to give you what you need.\n\nDoctor: When you need to, can you intentionally stop the tapping or freezing for a short time?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I try to. I really do. If a customer is looking at me funny, I try to just snap out of it. But... it's hard. \n\nLike I was saying, it just sort of happens. I'll be ringing someone up, and suddenly I'm just... stuck. You know, tapping the scanner in that little pattern, or just completely frozen with my hand hovering over the open cash drawer. Even with the customer standing right there, waiting for their change. \n\nI know I keep bringing that up, I'm sorry. I just... I really want to make sure you understand it's not something I'm doing on purpose to be difficult. I mean, it got me written up last week, which was so embarrassing. I've always been a good employee, you know? I really don't want my manager to think I'm slacking off or losing my touch. \n\nI'm sorry, did that answer your question? I feel like I'm talking in circles a bit, I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information. I really am trying my best to control it when it happens.\n\nDoctor: Did you ever have similar episodes like this when you were a child?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I don't think so. I mean, I was a pretty normal kid, you know? I always tried to be good, do my chores, get good grades. I didn't really have any... weird habits or anything like that back then. \n\nHonestly, this whole thing at the register is pretty new. It's just a recent little quirk, I guess. I'm sure it's just because I've been working so many shifts lately. Stress can do funny things to a person, right? \n\nI really hope this doesn't make me seem like I've always had something wrong with me. I've always been very capable and put-together. I just want to make sure you know that I'm usually very reliable. Does that make sense? I hope that's the kind of information you were looking for.\n\nDoctor: When one of these episodes happens, are you able to suppress it if you try, even for a short time?\n\nPatient: Um, well, I think we might have just touched on that, actually. Um, but to answer your question again, I really do try to stop it when I can. \n\nLike I was saying before, if a customer is waiting or looking at me, I try my absolute hardest to just push through it and snap out of it. But it's... it's just very difficult. Sometimes I just can't seem to stop the tapping or unfreeze myself right away, even if I'm trying to. \n\nI'm so sorry if I'm repeating myself, I really don't mean to be difficult or confusing. I just want to make sure I'm answering you correctly. I promise I'm trying my best to manage it at work. I hope that's okay?"
